- **An audit read its own report from the run before.** The reports go in the first folder given unless `-o` names another, and an audit of that folder run again read `audit.md` as a Markdown source, and `audit.html` and `audit.docx`, when they'd been asked for, as a page and a Word source: a finding on its own report each time, the report among its own inputs, and, since the report's date changes with each run, never "Nothing has changed since the audit of…". A report an audit wrote, known by its heading and the line that dates it, is passed over in a folder now, and one this audit writes isn't read even when it's named. A book's own file by the name of one the audit writes, a chapter named `audit.md` say, which it had written over, stops the audit before it writes anything ([Auditing](docs/auditing.md#the-report-and-when-its-stale)).
- **A frame in the footer setting was fetched**, outside an HTML page: reading the footer's HTML for an EPUB, a PDF, a Word file, or Markdown, Pandoc's HTML reader fetched the address a frame (`<iframe>`) named, as it does with `raw_html` off, and put what it got in the footer of every page, a request a front end checking strangers' settings mustn't make. The footer's Pandoc runs in its sandbox now, which refuses, and the frame is left out.
- **A presentation's slide faded its background in with reduced motion asked for.** reveal.js fades a slide's background in over 0.8 seconds, which `oer-reveal.css` didn't stop, as it stops the slides' own transitions; it stops both now, as the decks suite checks.
- **A file's own text came out of `audit.html` as markup.** The audit's report wrote a finding's detail, a file's name, and a PDF's metadata into `audit.md` as they were, and `--report-html` rendered it with Pandoc, which passes raw HTML through: a page's heading holding `<script>alert(1)</script>` or `<img src=x onerror="alert(2)">` was a script and an element in `audit.html`, as a PDF's title was. Turning raw HTML off isn't enough, since Markdown's own `[a link](javascript:alert(3))` is still a link, as Pandoc's manual says in its note on security; and a formula's TeX in a detail, raw TeX to Pandoc, was dropped from the page, `$x$` was math, and `--verbose` an en dash. What a file says is written into `audit.md` as text now, each character Markdown could read as markup escaped (`findings.md_text`, and a code span fenced longer than any run of backticks in it, `md_code`), and `audit.html` and `audit.docx` are rendered from it without raw HTML, raw TeX, or smart punctuation, in Pandoc's sandbox, the stylesheet given as a header of its own, which the sandbox reads. Measured with Pandoc 3.1.3 and 3.13. The audit suite's new case audits a page whose heading holds all of these and a PDF whose title holds a picture and a link, and checks `audit.md`, `audit.html`, and `audit.docx`; each of its five checks fails without the fix. [Auditing](docs/auditing.md#the-report-and-when-its-stale).
- **A page set in a CFF2 variable font showed no text**: the variable form of a font whose outlines are PostScript's, as Adobe's Source Serif 4 Variable is. luaotfload kept none of its outlines, so the PDF's font had no glyphs, though its text could still be copied. Its glyphs are drawn now, written as luaotfload's HarfBuzz mode writes them, each ending as a CFF glyph must, which that mode left off every glyph of such a face. A face with more than one axis is drawn as none of its instances, by that mode's reading of it, and the run warns, naming the face: its TrueType version, or its static faces, are drawn as they should be. [Configuration](docs/configuration.md#the-faces).
- **Acrobat said it couldn't extract a PDF's Noto Sans** ("Cannot extract the embedded font 'XKSRKX+NotoSans-Regular'. Some characters may not display or print correctly."), in *Introductory Business Statistics 2e*. A face with a STAT table that isn't a variable font, as Noto Sans 2.008's regular and bold are, was embedded as luaotfload embeds a variable font, from its own copy of the outlines: without the face's hinting, each accented letter drawn as one shape, and the glyphs its letter and accent are drawn from, which the PDF's font still holds, filled with others of the face's, a straight quote in place of the combining circumflex. Such a face is embedded from its own file now, as its italics were, which Acrobat didn't name, and Acrobat opens the book's PDF without the message. What Acrobat couldn't extract was the space, which a tagged PDF draws between words: luaotfload's copy gives an empty glyph an outline of no contours, where a font gives it none, and the book opened without the message with only those emptied, its hinting, its accented letters, and their parts as they were. A variable font, still embedded from luaotfload's copy, has its empty glyphs written empty, as a font writes them. The setting that does it is read before Pandoc's template sets the faces, from a copy of the template with it added, and the run warns where the template has changed so that it can't be; a LaTeX book's tagged copy, which its PDF is built from, reads it just after `\DocumentMetadata`, before its class. [Configuration](docs/configuration.md#the-faces).
- **A run of no-break spaces in text in a PDF's formula put a space character in the PDF's text for each**, side by side, which veraPDF's WCAG check 1.3.1-2 reports as spaces used to lay out the page, as PDF4WCAG did in *Introductory Business Statistics 2e*'s table of symbols, whose square root's sign is drawn over two rows of no-break spaces, six in the second: texmath writes such an `mtext` as `\text{~~~~~~}`, and tagpdf puts a space character in the text at each. A run keeps its width and has one space in the text, as a run of spaces between words in a paragraph has, its other spaces given none as LaTeX sets the formula, which leaves the formula's LaTeX as written and reaches a formula in raw LaTeX in a Markdown page too; the formula's MathML structure has the one space, and its attached MathML file the spaces as written. A formula of nothing but such spaces has none where a space stands beside it, the paragraph's spaces either side of it being two already. PDF4WCAG reports none in the book's PDF built so. [Formats](docs/formats.md#pdf).
- **A heading's bookmark in a PDF gave its formula as TeX**, "Power x^2 + y_1 \le \alpha", as Pandoc's writer makes a bookmark from its heading's text, in which a formula is its TeX. A formula is its text in the bookmark now, in Unicode where it can be, "Power x² + y₁ ≤ α": Pandoc's plain writer's text, with a fraction, a root, a limit, and an arrow or a line over a letter, which it can't write, written first, a/b, √x, lim_(x → a), a run of spaces one, and a script in Unicode's superscripts and subscripts where it can be, ℝⁿ. The heading, the contents, and the running head set it as before, and a latex target's LaTeX has it too. So does a deck's PDF, in a slide's bookmark and a section's. One that still can't be text, a matrix, a binomial, or a command texmath doesn't know, keeps its TeX, and a book's run says so once, in place of the plain writer's warning that it's rendered as TeX. [Formats](docs/formats.md#pdf), [Slides](docs/slides.md#the-pdf).
- **A heading's structure title in a PDF had a caret as a combining circumflex, and its contents entry's had its formulas' TeX.** LaTeX's tagging titles a heading's structure element, and its entry's in the contents, with its text made plain (`\pdf_purify:nN`), which made Pandoc's `\^{}` for a caret the combining circumflex, U+0302, on the character before it: "x̂q" for "x^q". It's a caret now, as in the bookmark. And a contents entry's title was its formulas' TeX, "Power $x^2 + y_1 \le \mitalpha $", as LaTeX writes the entry to its `.toc` file; it's the bookmark's text now, as the heading's title is. [Formats](docs/formats.md#pdf).
- **A title made from a heading gave a fraction, a root, or a limit in it as TeX, a piece's every formula, and an EPUB chapter's a formula's letters run together.** A page's `<title>`, its entry in the contents, a cartridge's name for it, a Word file's title, and a Markdown copy's front matter had Pandoc's plain writer's text of its heading's formulas, which gives one it can't write as its TeX between dollar signs, "Choosing $\frac{a}{b}$", and warned of each every time the page was filtered; a piece the split cut had its heading's TeX, "The \sqrt{x} section"; and an EPUB chapter had its heading's MathML's text, a/b's letters as "ab". Each has the formula's text now, as a PDF's bookmark has it (`bin/math-text.lua`): "Limits a/b and ℝⁿ", "The √x section". The heading keeps its formula, and a piece's name and its row in `page-names-new.csv` keep the TeX, as before. A formula that still can't be text, a matrix or a binomial, keeps its TeX between dollar signs, and the run says once which, in place of the plain writer's warnings. Each title has the formula as `math.repair_equations` repaired it, as the heading does (`bin/title-formulas.lua`, run after `math-repair.lua`, which makes a page's own title again of its heading as repaired), where a page's own had been of the formula as its source wrote it: a heading's `\overset{–}{X}`, X̄ on the page, was "X^(–)" in the page's title, and a Markdown copy of the page, read again, showed that title over its heading. [Formats](docs/formats.md#a-pages-title), [Splitting](docs/splitting.md#what-a-piece-is).
- **A Markdown copy whose title heading has a formula had two H1s when read again**: the copy declares the page's title in its front matter, and its heading, the formula as TeX, didn't say that title as the filter judges it, so the heading was shown as one of its own under the title, as with "Limits $\frac{a}{b}$ and ℝ^(n)" over "Limits $\frac{a}{b}$ and $\mathbb{R}^n$". An H1 is the title heading now where the declared title is its words with the formulas as text, too, as the copy writes it since the entry above, so the copy reads back with one. [Formats](docs/formats.md#a-pages-title).
- **A book's PDF build gave the book's pages as the PDF's**: its last line said "Wrote pdf/book.pdf: 2 page(s), 0 image(s), 0 without alternative text." of a PDF of 7 pages, the 2 being the book's pages, as an EPUB's line counts them. It counts the PDF's pages now, as LaTeX's last run says, and the book's apart: "Wrote pdf/book.pdf: 7 page(s); the book's 2 page(s) hold 0 image(s), 0 without alternative text." A deck's PDF and a LaTeX book's PDF from its own files counted the PDF's pages already.
- **A decision for a deck's table whose Fallback is a picture set the next table's Header Row**: a table in `mc:AlternateContent` is edited in its Choice and its Fallback, and where the Fallback was a picture of it, as no deck here has, the edit went on to the next table on the slide. Each copy of a table's frame is found within it.
- **A deck's title slide as LibreOffice saves one is its title slide**: a first slide that holds a title and a subtitle and nothing else, as LibreOffice writes a title slide, is the deck's title and subtitle, as one with PowerPoint's centered title is. Seven of eight Google Slides decks downloaded as ODP and saved as PowerPoint had their title slides read as ordinary first slides; the eighth's holds a title alone, and still is. A slide's layout doesn't decide it, since LibreOffice gives ordinary slides' layouts the Title Slide type too.
- **Pandoc 3.9 and 3.10, which the run takes, stopped it.** `convert.py`, `build-epub.py`, and a LaTeX book's reading passed Pandoc `--math-method=mathml`, an option 3.11 added, which 3.9 stops on ("Unknown option --math-method."); the table filter made every table's body with `pandoc.TableBody`, which Lua filters have had since 3.10; and a Markdown book with raw code in it stopped on 3.9 itself, whose `pandoc.read` takes a list only of its own source objects, where the Markdown reader gave it the files' names and texts, which 3.9.0.1 takes ("Source expected, got table"). Each call that writes a page, an EPUB, or a reveal.js presentation asks for MathML in the form the installed Pandoc takes (`lib/pandocversion.py`), `--mathml` before 3.11 and `--math-method=mathml` from it, and so do the ones that had left math to Pandoc's default, which is MathML only from 3.11 and text before it: the contents page and its menu, the Notes page, the header and the footer, and the raw HTML in a Markdown book, where 3.9 would have made a formula text. Where Pandoc has no `pandoc.TableBody`, the filter makes a body as 3.9's documentation does, a table of its four fields, and where it has no `pandoc.types.Sources`, which came with the wider `pandoc.read`, the Markdown reader gives it the files' texts as one string. The audit's page and Word file name their format, where Pandoc took it from the file's name, and the page asks for MathML too. Checked against 3.9's source, and by running every suite with a stand-in for it over 3.12.1, which takes 3.9's options, writes math as 3.9 does unless asked, and gives filters 3.9's functions, not with 3.9 itself ([Installation](docs/installation.md)).
- **An emoji in a PDF's text was a blank glyph**, which PDF/UA-2 forbids (veraPDF's 8.4.5.9-1): *Principles of Marketing*'s section 2.2 ends a video's title in a link with a globe, books, and a smiling face, only the last of which a fallback font had (DejaVu Sans), so the whole book's PDF failed the profile. Noto Emoji, the black-and-white one TeX Live's `noto-emoji` package has, is the last of the fallback fonts where it's installed, and the installation page installs it.
- **One malformed exercise stopped *Introduction to Python Programming* unpacking.** Its exercise CPL-ch05-m02-p03 leaves a tag open in OpenStax's exercise bank, `<code class="python" c< code data-lang="python">`, which a browser's parser reads as an attribute named `c<`, a name XML doesn't allow, and so does libxml2 2.14's, which lxml 6 has (2.12's reads `c`). Such an attribute is left out and reported (`exercise-html`), and so, by lxml's own rules, is what else XML doesn't allow, which no exercise in the bank has: an element named so (`<b<i>`), a span with its content kept, and a control character, left out; an exercise with something XML can't hold where nothing can be left out is a sentence where it would stand, so that one exercise can't stop a book. Found unpacking the 20 books with a repository here that embed exercises from the bank, offline, from a cache of the bank's exercises for them (16,553 exercises; [CNX input](docs/cnx-input.md#exercises-from-openstaxs-exercise-bank)), as are the next four.
- **488 formulas in the bank's exercises were left as TeX**, in the 20 books' pages, 382 of them in *Contemporary Mathematics*, 383 different ones: the bank writes them for MathJax, which takes forms Pandoc's TeX reader doesn't, plain TeX's font switches (`{\rm gallons}`), `\over`, MathJax's `\unicode`, `\mkern`, `\hfill`, a percent sign or a dollar sign as typed, and a line break outside an environment. A formula Pandoc can't read as written is read again with those written as LaTeX writes them (`lib/cnx/texforms.py`): a word in `\rm` as `\text`, so that MathML holds the word and not its letters, an operator between words math (`{\rm{maximum - minimum}}`, `\rm True \wedge False`); the rows of a one-column array that `\hfill` ends, as MathType aligns rows left and MathJax aligns them, aligned left; and the rest as LaTeX has them. All but one read so (`\fbox{\\}`, left as TeX and reported).
- **A line break just before the end of a formula's rows made an empty row**, in 50 of the bank's formulas, *Algebra 1*'s 15 and *Contemporary Mathematics*' 35, which end an `array` or `gathered` so (`a \\ b \\ \end{array}`): MathJax ends no row there, and Pandoc's reader reads one more, empty, which a screen reader may announce. It's left out of every formula of an exercise.
- **The programs in *Introduction to Python Programming*'s exercises weren't code in any format**, 274 of the code blocks in its pages: the bank writes them as a `pre` with no `code` in it, which Pandoc reads as text set apart from 3.11 on, each space a no-break space and each line a line break. Each is written as a page's own code is, its no-break spaces spaces, its indentation kept, its lines numbered where the question names them by number; a picture in one, which a code block can't hold, stands after the code before it (*Principles of Data Science*'s solutions, which its recipe leaves out, draw a plot so).
- **An exercise's picture whose address in the bank has no extension was copied as `.png` whatever it is**, 316 JPEGs and 80 SVGs of the 3,869 the 26 books' exercises show (*Algebra 1*'s 187 JPEGs and 80 SVGs, *Biology for AP® Courses*' 126 JPEGs, and *Contemporary Mathematics*' 3). A browser showed none of the SVGs, and a PDF stopped on the first ("libpng error: Not a PNG file"); and epubcheck rejected an EPUB holding either (OPF-029). Each is copied under the extension its bytes call for.
- **A Connexions archive formula whose function is named by markup lost the name.** Content MathML applying a function named by markup, *P* with a subscript *s* applied to *k* (`<apply><ci><msub>…</msub></ci><ci>k</ci></apply>`), was made a function with no name, "(*k*)", and not reported: 88 formulas of three of the archive's books (*Communication Systems* 37, *Multirate Signal Processing* 46, and *Discrete-Time Fourier Analysis* 5), and in a random sample of 150 of the archive's repositories, 607 formulas of six. Such a formula is left as written now, and reported (`content-mathml`), as a name made of markup is anywhere else in a formula. [A CNXML book as the source](docs/cnx-input.md).
- **Inline code ran past its line in a PDF, as far as off the paper**, a book's, a deck's, or the `latex` target's LaTeX built with LuaLaTeX. Pandoc's writer sets inline code with a language in fancyvrb's `\Verb`, which puts it in a box TeX can't break, where inline code in no language breaks at its spaces: *Introduction to Python Programming*'s 14.3 has a CSV file's text as one span, which ran 627 points past the text, 555 of them off the paper, and its Table 8.2, whose cells hold such code, was set at half its size and still ran past both of the paper's edges. The box is taken apart in its paragraph now (`latexbuild.CODE_SPACES` marks it, and `FIT_MACROS` takes it apart before TeX breaks the paragraph or the fit measures it), so the code breaks after a run of its spaces, the space kept at the line's end, and in a word after a run of marks where a letter or a digit follows, which TeX takes only where the word won't fit a line of its own, or, in text set flush to both margins, where moving it whole would leave the line before it too loose; between two letters only in a word longer than its line. The span sets in three lines inside the text, and the PDF's text is the code as written. Only where `\VERB` is as Pandoc's highlighting macros define it, the same in Pandoc 3.1.3, 3.12.1, and 3.13. Inline code in no language, which the writer sets as `\texttt` and TeX broke at its spaces alone, so that a word of it longer than its line ran on into the margin, breaks the same way (`pdf-target.lua` marks each span). [Formats](docs/formats.md#pdf).
- **A PDF's metadata file whose own LaTeX sets a math font, as a range of symbols from another font, stopped LaTeX where the file named no math font**, "No main maths font has been set up yet", since the default's IBM Plex Math was set after the file's LaTeX, and unicode-math takes a range only after a main math font; where the file named one but no text face, the build set it again after the file's LaTeX, which drops a range, and the range was lost without a word. The faces are set ahead of the file's LaTeX where it sets a math font, and the range is kept. [The faces](docs/configuration.md#the-faces).
- **A PDF's table cell gave a word less room than LaTeX sets it in**, where the cell sets it in from the column's edge, so that a word that filled its column broke or ran past it: a word of code that opens a row of a wrapped line, which hangs two characters in (below), a list's words, its margins in at each depth, and a description list's definition, its rows after the first in by the list's margin, and its term, set in bold in a box with the label space after it; and a table in a cell had the space at its own edges left out. The counts give such a word that room now, a list's margins and its label space measured as LaTeX sets a list in a table's cell, and bold text its bold face's widths, measured too, and so does LaTeX's own measure of a table whose columns it lays out, for a line of code; a line of code is what LaTeX hangs, each line of a cell by itself. A list's items had been counted as one text besides, an item's last word and the next's first one word, and a line of code's words each with the space after it, which a row that breaks there takes back, each of which set a column wider than it needed and its table smaller. *Introduction to Python Programming*'s Table 14.2 holds its words of code whole now, at 93 percent of its size, where it was at 96 and broke one; on ten pages of *Nutrition for Nurses* whose tables' cells hold 319 lists, none of ten words in its tables that broke at a row's end breaks, and 2 tables are set smaller to fit, where 6 were, the smallest at 75 percent of its size, where it was at 65. A column held at what its longest word needs has its part of the line written as the least of the writer's four places that TeX, reading it to the nearest 65,536th, takes as no less than the word needs, where it was rounded to the nearest of four places, which left a column held at a word of code a few scaled points too narrow for it; the page's measures are taken to six places for it, where they were taken to four. [Formats](docs/formats.md#pdf).
- **A LaTeX book's image scaled with `\includegraphics`' `scale` was at its natural size** in the pages and everything made from them, since Pandoc's LaTeX reader drops the key: GIAM's photograph of a row of dominoes, which it sets at a fifth of its size, was five times as wide. It's written as the width graphicx gives the image, in inches, from the image's natural size as LuaLaTeX's graphicx reads it (a PDF's crop box, an EPS's bounding box, a PNG's or JPEG's pixels at its resolution, measured against LuaLaTeX's own), and left as it is beside a width, a height, an angle, or a box of the image's own. [A LaTeX book as the source](docs/latex.md).
- **A PDF's paragraph of code line by line wrapped a long line at the margin**, where its later parts read as lines of their own: *Introduction to Python Programming*'s 7.4 sets the output of `help()` so, each line of code ended by a line break, and each function's description ran on under the function's name. Each part of such a line after its first is set the line's indentation and two characters further in now, the arrow a block of code's wrapped line has before it, and a line's parts are kept on one page, as a block's are; so is a paragraph of one line of code, and a table's cell of lines of code, which the writer sets as a paragraph a line. The arrow is an artifact in a tagged PDF, and the PDF's text is the code as written. A line in no language keeps its indentation, which TeX had taken out at the start of a row after a line break. [Formats](docs/formats.md#pdf).
- **A formula set as LaTeX's `array` was measured with its padding, 5 points blank at either edge**, which LaTeX's `array` sets before its first column and after its last, so a PDF's fit took it for wider than it is: texmath writes an `mtable` that's no matrix, a worked example's steps among them, as an `array`, and *College Algebra 2e*'s Example 12 (e) was reported 0.6 points wider than its line at three quarters of its size while its ink ended 1.4 points inside it, and a formula as wide as its line less the padding was set smaller than it needed to be. Each edge of such an array with no rule at it has none now (`@{}`), as amsmath's matrices, which texmath writes for a centered `mtable`, have none, but for an array right after a delimiter, which the padding keeps off its cells, inside `\left` and `\right` or after a delimiter MathML doesn't stretch (`\lbrack\begin{array}`), and one inside another's cell. Example 12 (e) fits at three quarters of its size in Latin Modern Math, its ink 3.4 points inside the line, and isn't reported. [Formats](docs/formats.md#pdf).
- **A LaTeX book's PDF and EPS images and its drawings were three quarters of their size** in the pages and a PDF from them, where `pdftocairo` from poppler 24.02, Ubuntu 24.04's, made their SVGs: it writes an SVG's size, the PDF page's in points, as a bare number, which an SVG reads as pixels, with cairo 1.17.6 or later. The run puts `pt` on it, on an SVG it made before too; poppler 26.07 writes `pt` itself ([Ubuntu's bug 2127078](https://www.mail-archive.com/desktop-bugs@lists.ubuntu.com/msg827282.html)). The LaTeX suite's checks of the images and of a PDF from a book's pages now fail without it, where poppler is 24.02.
- **The `latex` target of a book whose one page has the book's name**, as a single LaTeX file has when `project.yaml` names nothing (`book.tex`'s page and identifier are both `book`), wrote its master over the page, and the master `\include`d itself: the page is written apart, `book-text.tex`.
- **A PDF's table with a block of code or a table in a cell gave that cell's column too little room.** The PDF target counts each column's longest word to widen a column narrower than it, and a block of code, whose lines TeX sets whole, and a table, which TeX doesn't break, each counted as nothing, leaving the column the share Pandoc's reader gave every column: in *Introduction to Python Programming*'s section 15.3, Tables 15.2 and 15.4, each with code and a DataFrame's printed table in its cells, were set at half their size and still ran off the page, 302 points wider than the text, and are set at 70 percent of their size or more now, centered, 97 points wider at most. A line of a block of code counts as a word, the block the cell's or inside a division in it, and a table in a cell as one as wide as its columns' widest cells and the spaces between them, the columns a cell spans widened to hold it. [Formats](docs/formats.md#pdf).
- **An OpenStax paragraph that holds nothing but code of more than one line ran its lines together.** *Introduction to Python Programming*'s Table 8.2 has a program and its output in its cells so, each code of the class `inline-code` in a paragraph of its own, which a reader of HTML ran together on one line, the run of spaces its output is about one. The CNX unpacker makes such a paragraph a code block, the paragraph's id and language its own and the class `inline-code` dropped, unless the code says it's inline or the paragraph has text after a comment; the code's own id, where it has one, is the block's, and the paragraph's is then a division's around it; the table keeps its lines, set at three quarters of its size in a PDF, centered, 82 points wider than the text. [CNX input](docs/cnx-input.md).
- **A list's item that opens with highlighted code lost its number in a tagged PDF**, a book's or a deck's: the number was set over the next item's ("2." over "3."), where an untagged PDF has it beside the code's first line. LaTeX's tagging code keeps an item's label until the item's first paragraph begins, and fancyvrb, which Pandoc sets highlighted code in, begins none: it sets the label from where LaTeX kept it before the tagging code, which is empty, and marks it set. The label is moved there first now (`latexbuild.PDF_MACROS`, which the `latex` target's LaTeX carries too), and the number is beside the code's first line, tagged as untagged; the PDF's structure, which had the label in its own item all the same, is unchanged. Code in no language, which LaTeX's own verbatim sets a line to a paragraph, kept its number. A LaTeX book's tagged copy, and the PDF built from it, gets the same where the book loads fancyvrb, or fvextra or minted, which load it, and the run says so.
- **A formula a saved page held as MathJax 2 drew it from MathML was lost.** MathJax 2 keeps such a formula's source after its rendering, in a `<script type="math/mml">`, and the run dropped the rendering beside it as it does one beside MathJax's TeX, which Pandoc reads; Pandoc reads no other script, and the conversion drops the rest, so neither was read. The script's MathML takes its place now, and the run counts the formulas read from it. A rendering beside an AsciiMath script, which Pandoc doesn't read either, is read from the rendering, where it had been dropped too. [HTML sources](docs/html.md).
- **A suite failed checks for a requirement that isn't installed, where the testing page says such a check skips.** On a machine without pikepdf, `rsvg-convert`, poppler-utils, and Ghostscript, the slides suite failed its check of a deck's PDF, which it read with pikepdf, and the LaTeX suite 11, its first naming `rsvg-convert` alone: seven of the latex target, whose book's SVG picture its build and its reading back couldn't take without `rsvg-convert`, one of a figure's fonts, which Ghostscript or `pdftocairo` embeds, and two of a Word file read back, which were the code's (below). A TeX without a package the books or their PDFs load failed the LaTeX, PDF, and decks suites' first checks and the checks of each book or deck it stopped, and a LaTeX too old for the PDF target failed the LaTeX suite's first check and its PDFs'. Each is a machine to set up, and the checks that need it skip now, saying what's missing and what installs it: the LaTeX suite's first lines name what TeX lacks, or its LaTeX's age, and a missing `rsvg-convert`, and its checks that build with TeX, or that need an SVG made a PDF, skip, as they do without TeX; without `rsvg-convert` the latex target's book has no SVG picture, so the rest of its checks run, and without `pdftocairo` its reading back, whose picture is then a PDF, skips. The PDF suite skips whole, as it does without LuaLaTeX, the decks suite skips its checks of a deck's PDF, and the slides suite, which checks for what its deck's PDF loads now (`pict2e`, `luacolor`, and `lua-ul`), skips building the PDF without them and reading its slides without pikepdf. Each also checks for what every tagged PDF loads, LaTeX's tagging code (`latex-lab`, `tagpdf`) and `luamml`, and the LaTeX and PDF suites for `selnolig` and `luatexbase`, which Pandoc's template loads for a book with a language. And without pypdf the audit suite's checks of a PDF and the unit suite's of what a PDF has where veraPDF's checks failed, and without html5lib and lxml the convert suite's check of a browser's save in a zip, skip too, where they had failed. A case run with Pandoc and Python alone on its PATH runs Python through a script now, not a link, since a venv's Python run through a link starts without the venv's packages.
- **A LaTeX book's PDF or EPS image that couldn't be made SVG was kept as it was, and nothing was said**, as on a machine without poppler-utils: the page embedded the PDF, and the Word file held an image Word can't show, which stopped the run that read the Word file back. It's left out of the pages now, as the LaTeX page says, and of everything made from them, the `latex` target and a PDF built from the pages among them, and the run says so, naming the images and what installs the programs they need (`pdftocairo`, and for an EPS image `epstopdf` and Ghostscript too), or, where a program couldn't read a file, the error it gave, each image tried once. The PDF a LaTeX book builds from its own LaTeX keeps it. One in the master's preamble, in `\title` or a macro without arguments, had been made an SVG and then kept as the book wrote it, so a page embedded it even with `pdftocairo`, and one whose options held a `]` in braces (`alt={A [bracketed] plot}`) had been passed over; each is made an SVG, or left out, as any other is. So is one behind a macro of the book's that calls its own macro for an image (`\newcommand{\Fig}[1]{\Image{2cm}{img/#1}}`), which had been left as the call wrote it, unconverted and unnamed.
- **A PDF stopped on a definition list's term with an `=` in its words**, with a Pandoc before 3.13: its LaTeX writer leaves the term bare in `\item`'s optional argument, which LaTeX's tagging reads as keys and stops on as keys it doesn't know ("Some keys specified on the \item command are unknown"), the PDF not built. `pdf-target.lua` braces such a term, as 3.13's writer does ([#11954](https://github.com/jgm/pandoc/issues/11954)); a deck's PDF and a `latex` target's master go through the same filter. Measured with 3.12.1 and 3.13 and LaTeX 2026-06-01.
- **With Pandoc 3.13, a list read back from a Word target came apart.** 3.13's Word writer gives a list item's second paragraph, code, or quotation no number, only the item's indent, where it had numbered each in a list of its own with a blank marker, and its reader keeps a paragraph so indented in the item and ends the list at anything else ([#9197](https://github.com/jgm/pandoc/issues/9197)). A formula in an item, which the target still numbered as the older writer did, ended the item, and so did a table or a rule in it, a note, whose paragraphs the reader reads where its reference is, and a figure, which the reader pairs with its caption now that its picture isn't numbered, its caption's words read as a quotation: what followed in the item came back as quotations outside it, and the list's later items as a list of their own, numbered from 1 again. The words of an item that opens with an anchor before a list, which the writer numbers nowhere, came back as a quotation, and so did what follows a heading in an item. Code in an item's quotation, a quotation in that quotation, and code in a quoted list's item came out of their items too, and a quoted list item's second paragraph came back quoted again. The target follows the writer it runs now, giving a formula in an item the item's indent with 3.13, and, told by a document variable it writes (`TextbookImproverContinuations`), it reads such a file back with an item's quotations and code lined up with the item's text, so Pandoc's reader keeps them in the item, the paragraphs after a table, a rule, a figure, or a note kept in it, the table, rule, or figure put back in the item, and a paragraph lined up with no list the reader keeps open read as a paragraph of the list, as before; a file written with either Pandoc reads back with either ([Word output](docs/formats.md#word-output)). So a figure in an item comes back a figure from a file written with 3.13, one of two pictures too, which the writer writes as a table; one written before, or one that opens its item, whose picture the writer numbers, gives its picture, and its caption as a paragraph. And with either Pandoc, a table with a caption in a list item came out of the list: the writer gives its caption the item's style, which the reader took for a paragraph beside the caption it gave the table from its alternative text, the caption twice. The target gives the caption its own style now, Table Caption, and reading a file written before makes the paragraph the table's caption. An item that opens with an anchor before a list, as OpenStax's solutions open their parts, got no number from either writer, so it was lost and its list read as the item before's; its anchors are a paragraph of their own now, which the writer numbers. An item that opens with a table or a rule got no number either, the file numbering the item after it in its place, and the list came back split there, the table or rule out of it; it gets an empty paragraph first now, as the writer gives an item that opens with a list. A formula alone in a tight list's item, or alone in a span, ended the list with either Pandoc, the target taking only a paragraph's for one; a `Plain`'s is one too, as the writer takes it. And a quotation in a list item could come back with an empty anchor, `tiqItemQuote1x1`, the reading's own mark, on a page where the reading changed nothing else; it's taken off now. With either Pandoc too, a list in a list item came apart where both items held a table or a rule, or the outer item had a paragraph after the inner list: the inner item's table came back in the outer item, and the outer list's later items as a list of their own. The reading counts an item's blocks after what it holds as Pandoc's reader gives them back now, a list the item holds split where its items hold a table too, and puts the outer item's back first. Measured on *Introductory Business Statistics 2e* through Word and back.
- **A table of 10 columns or more with a line break or a block in a cell was lost from a Markdown target.** Pandoc's Markdown writer before 3.13 writes such a table as a grid table only where the line has room for 8 characters a column, 72 by default, and a wider one, with raw HTML off as the target has it, as the text `[TABLE]`, saying so only with `--verbose`. With such a Pandoc the target writes that table as a fenced HTML block now, which reads back as the table it was, as a table with merged cells is written; 3.13's writer writes it as a grid table at any width ([#11945](https://github.com/jgm/pandoc/issues/11945)).
- **A source's copy of an HTML or a Markdown page kept none of the alt text decided for its pictures in the book's own `media/` folder.** The run keys such a picture under its page's name, as it keys a Word file's media, and by its path as Pandoc's reader writes it, a space as `%20` (`3-2-add-integers/media/my%20pic.png`), which is how `image-alt-missing.csv` gives it. The pages took that row, and the copy looked for the path alone, so the author's copy of a book the CNX unpacker wrote kept none of the decisions its pages had. A copy finds the row as the pages do now, and one keyed by the path alone still. [Sidecars](docs/sidecars.md).
- **A picture's alt text in a PDF kept TeX in it.** Pandoc's writer writes a straight quote in an image's alt key as `\textquotesingle`, a bar, `<`, `>`, `~`, and a backslash as commands named for them, `^` as `\^{}`, and curly quotes and dashes as TeX's ligatures (`` `` ``, `''`, `--`), and LaTeX's tagging made the figure's `/Alt` from the key's text made plain, which kept the commands' names and the ligatures as typed: 96 of the 224 alt texts in *Prealgebra 2e*'s third chapter read "\textquotesingle" or "\textbar" ("the \textquotesingle 8\textquotesingle  colored in red"), and of OpenStax's 43,153 alt texts, 3,783 have a straight quote and 3,110 a curly double quote. Each is the character the alt text has now, in a book's PDF and a deck's, and the six commands are their characters wherever else LaTeX makes text plain too; a backquote is a left quote, as TeX sets one, the writer writing the two alike. [The outputs](docs/formats.md#pdf).
- **The PDF suite left a folder in the temporary directory on every run**, `pdf-tests-…-languages`, the book of pages in other languages it has built since f3ac5cc: its clean-up and its `--keep` message each named the folders its books are built in, and neither named that one. Both read one list now, and a check sees that every folder a book is built in is on it. [Testing](docs/testing.md).
- **A formula whose TeX opens with `\displaystyle` was in text style on a page, in an EPUB, and in a deck's presentation.** texmath, which writes their MathML, reads TeX's `\displaystyle` as nothing, so such a formula's fractions and large operators were drawn small and its limits as scripts, where a PDF draws them full size, a sum's limits under and over it. Its MathML is in an `<mstyle displaystyle="true">` now, laid out as Pandoc lays out a display formula but in the line, its TeX the annotation as before, so a page reads back as it was written (`target-blocks.lua`). One in a heading or a picture's description stays in text style, its TeX the text the writer reads from it, a picture's alt text and an EPUB's contents. A Word file sets it as before: Pandoc's writer writes the same Office math with `\displaystyle` as without. [The outputs](docs/formats.md).
- **`math.from_text` made an equation of the start of a formula it couldn't take whole.** OpenStax's ANOVA deck types `F = MS(Factor)/MS(Error)`, and its first part, `F = MS`, became an equation, the rest text after it. A run followed straight away by an opening parenthesis or a letter stays text now, as `x = 3cm` does too. [Math](docs/configuration.md#math).
- **A sidecar saved by Excel, or with a report appended to it, wasn't read as written.** Excel's "CSV UTF-8" puts a byte-order mark at a file's start, and appending a report that starts with one, its header row and all, leaves one at a line's start. The filter and some of the Python dropped only the first, and the rest neither: `page-names.csv` saved by Excel named no page at all; `table-headers.py` warned of a header row's `headers` value and set the row aside as matching no table, writing `table-headers-unmatched.csv` and `table-headers-sample.csv` for it; the run named a header row of `bare-links.csv` a row matching no bare link; a header row after a mark was a row of `languages.csv` whose language isn't a tag, a slide of `slide-titles.csv` no deck has, and an order of `reading-order.csv` that isn't a list of ids; and a row after a mark was keyed on the mark and its key, so its value applied to nothing. Every reader drops the mark at any line's start now (`lib/sidecars.py`). And a header row of `math-keep.csv` is skipped wherever it is, as one of `image-alt.csv` is, where one after the first was math that kept nothing, warned of; so is one of `image-alt.csv` or `bare-links.csv` where an HTML copy reads them, one of `bare-links.csv` where a Word copy does, and one of `page-names.csv`, each of which had been a row.
- **A sidecar that isn't UTF-8 stopped the run with a traceback**, and `math-keep.csv` and the filter's (`image-alt.csv`, `table-captions.csv`, `bare-links.csv`) only after the pages were written, the filter's with each character it couldn't read a U+FFFD there. Excel's plain "CSV" saves in the computer's code page, where an en dash is a byte that isn't UTF-8. Now the run rewrites such a sidecar as UTF-8 before anything is converted, whether or not the run reads it, and so do `table-headers.py`, `split-pages.py`, `util/remediate.py`, `util/round-trip-decks.py`, and `util/shortdoi.py` (`sidecars.rewrite_as_utf8`), but for `shortdoi.py --dry-run`, which writes nothing. Its lines that aren't UTF-8 are read in Windows-1252 or in Mac OS Roman, the code pages Windows Excel and Excel for Mac use in the US and Western Europe, whichever has fewer characters misplaced ("donÕt" is "don’t" read in the wrong one), and where they have as many, by the line ends, a carriage return alone being Mac OS Roman's; a line that is UTF-8, as a report's rows appended to it are, is kept as it is; the file as it was is kept beside it (`image-alt.csv.windows-1252`); the new one is written beside it and moved into its place, its owner and mode kept, so a write that fails leaves it as it was; and the run says so, showing the lines as now read, to check, as it can read one in the wrong code page (`docs/sidecars.md` says when). A file whose lines all read with a character misplaced in each code page, as most saved in Cyrillic or Greek do, stops the run instead, saying how each reads them and to save the file as "CSV UTF-8", and so do a control character and a file in UTF-16, as Excel's "Unicode Text" saves one. One the run can't rewrite, a read-only file say, or on Windows one that Excel has open, as Excel is reported to lock a file there (not tried here), is read in its code page all the same, and left as it is (under Breaking). A sidecar setting naming a folder stops the run too, saying so. [A sidecar that isn't UTF-8](docs/sidecars.md#a-sidecar-that-isnt-utf-8).
- **A deck's PDF bookmarked a slide whose title has two hyphens with an en dash.** hyperref's `\pdfstringdef`, which writes a bookmark, takes `--` and `---` for dashes and `` ?` `` and `` !` `` for inverted marks, where the slide prints its title's hyphens as they are; its bookmark keeps them now, as a link's description does. [Slides](docs/slides.md#the-pdf).
- **A link in a caption put its `/Contents` and its address in the title of the caption's entry in a PDF's list of figures or tables.** LaTeX titles the entry's structure element with the entry's text made plain, which kept both: "has a linked wordhttps://example.org/captiona linked word in it", and a link within the book left its target's name in brackets. The title is the caption's words now, a link's text, a linked citation's too, without its target or its `/Contents`. [Formats](docs/formats.md#pdf).
- **In a PDF/UA-1 book, a link LaTeX makes on its own got no description after a link in a footnote, a table's cell, or a caption.** `\pdfstringdef` sets a link's description for the whole document, and the command after each link cleared it for the group the link was in alone, so once that group ended the description was set again, and the plug that describes a link LaTeX makes on its own, a contents line or a `\url` in the book's own LaTeX, took it to be the link's own and gave none, which PDF/UA-1 requires (veraPDF's 7.18.5-2). It's cleared for the whole document now. [Formats](docs/formats.md#pdf).
- **A Word target's file was in US English whatever the book's language**, its reference document's, for a page from a Word file, which Pandoc's reader gives no language: it's in the page's language now, the book's for a page in the book's.
- **A PDF with a formula holding an empty row wasn't built, or lost the row.** texmath writes a one-column `mtable` whose last row is empty with an empty line before its end, as OpenStax writes an evaluation bar's limits in *Calculus*, a synthetic division's divisor in *Precalculus 2e*, and an empty row or two as space beside a matrix or a derivation there and in *College Algebra 2e*. LaTeX's tagging read the line as a paragraph's end and stopped inside a display formula ("Paragraph ended before \environment equation* was complete"): *Calculus Volume 2*'s chapter 3, with three such display formulas, and *Precalculus 2e*'s chapter 7, with two of the book's 19. Where LaTeX read on, in a formula in text or an untagged PDF's display, the row was lost, so a bar's upper limit sat 9.5 points above the formula's line and its lower one 28.5 below. The PDF target writes the empty line as the row it stands for, an empty group: the chapters build, a bar's limits are 27.6 points above its formula's line and 28.5 below, and a synthetic division's divisor is on its first row, not between the first two. [PANDOC-NOTES](notes/PANDOC-NOTES.md#the-html-reader).
- **A formula holding an empty row lost it in a `markdown` or `asciidoc` target.** texmath writes an `mtable`'s empty last row as an empty line before the matrix's end (above). The Markdown reader ends a paragraph there, so a `markdown` target's page read back with an evaluation bar's formula as the text `$|_{`, raw TeX, and `}$`; and the `asciidoc` target, which writes a formula in text on one line, wrote `\\ \end{matrix}`, which texmath reads as the row but LaTeX reads as none. Both write the row as an empty group, `{}`, as the PDF target does, so a page reads back with each formula whole and its empty rows kept. [PANDOC-NOTES](notes/PANDOC-NOTES.md#the-html-reader).
- **A link's description in a PDF had an en dash where its address has two hyphens.** The PDF target writes each link's `/Contents` through hyperref's `\pdfstringdef`, which takes `--` and `---` for dashes and `` ?` `` and `` !` `` for inverted marks, as TeX's text does, so *Introduction to Sociology 3e*'s two addresses at ilo.org, whose path names the page's language (`…/lang--en/index.htm`), were described as `lang–en`, which isn't the address; the link itself, and the address printed, were right. A description keeps its hyphens and backticks as they're written now, and so, in a PDF/UA-1 book or deck, does the address that describes a link LaTeX makes on its own. [Formats](docs/formats.md#pdf).
- **A PDF's floating table was tagged at the end of the document**, after everything else a screen reader reads. Pandoc's writer sets a table of the class `float` in a `table` float, not a longtable, as a page of two columns needs, and it floats under every `pdf.figures` placement, which placed only figures where the text has them; LaTeX's tagging gathered its tags at the end, or under `section` after its section, in its chapter. In a book with one between two paragraphs of its first chapter, the table's tags came after the second chapter's. The PDF target now tags every float where the text has it (latex-lab's `float/here`), a figure under any placement too, as a LaTeX book's tagged copy already did; where each is printed doesn't change ([PDF](docs/formats.md#pdf)).
- **The audit named a file renamed since its last audit by its old name, and filed a copy's findings under the original's.** Its cache kept a file's findings by the file's SHA-256 alone, so a page renamed from `draft.html` to `final.html` had its findings under `draft.html`, in the report and in `audit.csv`, and an audit with nothing else changed said the report was current; and a copy of a file had the findings of whichever of the two was read last, under that one's name. Nor was an entry ever replaced, so the cache grew with each build of each file. A file's findings are kept by where it is now, with its SHA-256, a file made again replacing its own, so a file renamed or copied is read again, under its own name. An entry kept the old way isn't read, and is dropped, so the first audit of a folder after this reads every file again. [Auditing](docs/auditing.md#the-report-and-when-its-stale).
- **`--report-html` or `--report-docx` with nothing changed since the last audit wrote neither**, saying `audit.md` was current, when the report before hadn't been written in that form, or its file was gone since. The forms a report is written in are recorded with it, and one asked for that wasn't, or isn't there, has the report made again. [Auditing](docs/auditing.md#the-report-and-when-its-stale).
- **An audit with nothing else changed took its report for current after veraPDF was installed, upgraded, or taken away**, so a PDF's claims were left unverified, or verified by a veraPDF no longer there. What a PDF's own findings turn on is kept with them now, and an audit whose PDFs' checks turn on another veraPDF makes the report again. [Auditing](docs/auditing.md#the-report-and-when-its-stale).
- **The audit took a report made with other options for the one asked for.** With nothing changed since, `audit.py` without `--quick` said the report made with it was current, and served a page read with `--quick` from its cache to a run without it, so the validators never saw the page. The options are recorded with the report, `--wcag`'s version, level, best practices, experimental rules, and `--verbose` among them, `--quick` with each file's findings too, and a run with others reads again. A cache written before this reads everything again, once.
- **The installation page's commands for veraPDF kept the old version when run to upgrade it**, if an older download was still in `/tmp/verapdf`: the installer's wildcard matched both, and Java ran the older one, which reinstalled itself without a word (measured, 1.30.2 to 1.30.3). They clear the folder first now, and the page says how to upgrade. [Installation](docs/installation.md#optional-verapdf).
- **A formula's term struck out was drawn with a font that maps it to no character**, in a book's PDF and a deck's, and in a LaTeX book's tagged copy: the cancel package, which the template loads for `\cancel`, `\bcancel`, and `\xcancel` (texmath writes them for MathML's diagonal strikes, as OpenStax's math books have them), draws its strokes with glyphs of LaTeX's `line10` font, whose map to Unicode has only a space, and veraPDF failed *College Algebra 2e*'s first chapter on two of them (8.4.5.8, in PDF/UA-2 and both WTPDF profiles). pict2e, loaded where cancel is, draws them as paths, each where it was, and a book's own command named as one of pict2e's drawing commands (`\arc`, `\Line`) is the book's. So a book or a deck that strikes a term out needs TeX's `pict2e` package as well as `cancel`, neither of which TinyTeX or TeX Live's medium scheme has: the installation page's first `tlmgr` line installs both, and when either is missing, the run's advice names both. [PDF](docs/formats.md#pdf), [Installation](docs/installation.md#everything-at-once).
- **A box's rule above it could stay at the foot of a page, its box on the next**, when the box opened with a heading, as a LibreTexts page's Example opens with its legend: LaTeX gives a heading a place to break a page before it, which came after the rule. The rule now goes with the heading, as a heading goes with what follows it, and the heading sits a medium space under it, without the space a heading has above it.
- **A page number of four digits ran into the margin of a PDF's contents**, as a book of a thousand pages has: the standard classes leave room for three. The contents and a list of figures leave room for four: *Introduction to Sociology 3e* in 13.5-point type had 62 such lines, a section's number each, where a chapter's ran back toward its title, which LaTeX doesn't report.
- **A PDF's contents and bookmarks went a level deeper than `pdf.toc_depth` says.** The setting counts levels as an EPUB's contents do, a group at the top of `project.contents` being level 1, and the build gave it to Pandoc's template as it stood, for LaTeX's `tocdepth`, which counts a chapter as 0; Pandoc's writer takes one off its own `--toc-depth` for a class with chapters, but not off a `toc-depth` in the metadata. So at the default, 2, a book's contents and bookmarks took in its pages' subsections: *Introduction to Sociology 3e*'s contents had 1,030 entries on 24 pages, where its EPUB's have 295, and its preface began on page xxvii, so that the contents' page numbers for the preface's sections, xxvii to xxxiii, were too wide for their column. A class with chapters takes one less now: Sociology's contents are 8 pages, and its preface begins on page xi. A `toc-depth` a `pdf.metadata` file sets is LaTeX's own count, as it was, which the setting's description now says. And the bookmarks follow the setting with no contents page, where a `pdf.metadata` file's `toc: false` had left them the class's own depth, a level deeper: Pandoc's template sets LaTeX's depth only where it writes the contents. [Conversion settings](docs/conversion-settings.md#pdf).
- **A face a `pdf.metadata` file named had no fallback fonts**, so a character it lacks was left out of the PDF, where the default's faces have Latin Modern Math, DejaVu Sans, and New Computer Modern's math font to fill in: Noto Sans has no ≤, and IBM Plex no ⇒. A named face has them now, unless the file gives it fallbacks of its own (Pandoc's `mainfontfallback`), babel's copy of the face for the book's language as well as fontspec's. A face that isn't installed stops LaTeX as it did, and the run now names it and says where to get it. [PDF](docs/formats.md#pdf).
- **`build-pdf.py` run on its own with a relative `-d` couldn't find the `pdf.metadata` file** ("Could not find metadata file 'book/meta.yaml'"): Pandoc reads it in the book's folder, and was given its path relative to where the build ran.
- **`build-pdf.py` run on its own on a LaTeX book wrote the default page**, `--latex-only` say, and none of the book's colors, on which LaTeX stops: `convert.py` passes them, and the script had no other way to know them. It reads them from the book's masters now, as `convert.py` does, when they aren't passed.
- **An EPUB failed epubcheck on an SVG picture holding an `id` more than once** (RSC-005): *Algebra 1*'s graph from Desmos gives each of its three expressions the same one, which nothing refers to. The EPUB's copy of such a picture has the repeats renamed, the first kept, and the run says how many; an id the picture's own stylesheet names, which styles every element that has it, is left, and the run warns of it. [EPUB](docs/epub.md).
- **A table whose `<thead>` held every row was all header cells, with no body, in every output.** LibreTexts' editor writes tables so, putting rows of data in the head with the header row: 63 of *Instrumental Analysis*'s, Table 20.2.2 among them. Pandoc's HTML reader makes every row in a `<thead>` a header row, whatever its cells, so a page's and an EPUB's table was a head of `<th>` and nothing else, which an accessibility checker reports as "missing body content", every cell of the PDF's was a TH, and a Word file repeated every row as a header on every page. Before reading, the rows from the first holding a data cell (a `<td>` with something in it) are made the table's body, and the rows of header cells before them stay its head. A `<thead>` with a body after it is left as its author divided it: *Instrumental Analysis*'s Table 26.4.1 heads its columns with a row of `<td>`, which stays a header row. [HTML sources](docs/html.md).
- **A mark holding nothing but a space was an empty Span in a tagged PDF and two spaces in a Word file, and a cell holding nothing but a no-break space a space character in a PDF.** Editors leave white space where there's nothing to set: a subscript holding just the space typed after it (`<sub> </sub>`), and an empty table cell holding `&nbsp;` so an old browser drew its border. Pandoc's HTML reader takes a space at a mark's edge out of it, on each side, so the subscript became a space, an empty subscript, and a space: a Word file showed two spaces, and LaTeX's tagging made a Span with nothing in it, which veraPDF's WCAG 2.2 profile (PDF4WCAG's) reports as a redundant span (4.1.2-17). The cell's space was a character in the PDF's text, so a row of such cells was a run of spaces, which the profile takes for spaces used for formatting (1.3.1-2). *Instrumental Analysis* had 12 such subscripts, and 11 rows of nine such cells in its table of the normal distribution. Emphasis, bold, a subscript, a superscript, small capitals, or a span with no attributes holding nothing but white space is that white space now, an underline or a strikeout holding nothing at all goes, and a table's cell holding nothing but white space is empty, in every output; the two spaces such a mark leaves side by side are one, inside another mark or not, as are those a link with no text leaves when it's dropped and a space at a mark's edge beside another, though no mark is emptied to make them one. Word had shown each as two: 212 of the 218 in *Instrumental Analysis*'s Word files, the other 6 inside formulas. [Architecture](docs/architecture.md#what-conversion-does-to-your-pages).
- **A PDF's table whose longest words were wider than a line ran past the page's edge, and a column narrower than its longest formula ran into the next.** A formula's longest word had been the whole formula, where LaTeX breaks one in text after a relation or an operator, so a column of reactions counted as unbreakable: its longest word is now its longest piece, the line of a `gathered` that can't break at all. A table whose longest words don't fit a line is set as narrow as it can be, and fitted to the line as LaTeX sets it (above). A table with widths, its source's or the equal ones Pandoc's HTML reader gives a table whose cells hold more than a paragraph, has a column narrower than its longest word or formula widened to it, from the columns with room to spare. A fill-in blank counts as three underscores in a word, the shortest it's drawn to fit its column: counted whole, a long blank's column took the line from the next column's longest word, and the table was set smaller for it, one of two columns half the line each, a word of 26 letters in one and a blank of 64 underscores in the other, at 86 percent of its size. Underscores in code, which LaTeX doesn't draw shorter, count whole. On *Instrumental Analysis*: Table 23.4.1's reactions ran into the next column by up to 77 points, and fit; Table 24.3.1 ran past the page's edge by 165 points, and is fitted as above. The build names what's still too wide (above).
- **Pictures a page sets side by side stood one under another**, in the pages, the EPUB, and the PDF, though *Instrumental Analysis*'s captions say which is left and which right: a figure holding a row of its grid (`<figure><div class="row"><figure>…`, as LibreTexts sets two). The pages lay such a row out side by side, one picture under another in a narrow window (`page.css`), and the PDF sets them in one line, each its share of it, or its own width where that's less.
- **A box was drawn only in the pages and the EPUB.** A box, a div of class `box` (a LibreTexts page's Example, Exercise, or Note, a LaTeX book's framed passage), is set off in the PDF by a rule above it and one below, each an artifact (veraPDF passes it), and in a Word file by a border around its paragraphs, which Word draws as one; a table in it keeps its own. Where a box ended had been the next heading's guess.
- **A `toc: false` in the `pdf.metadata` file left a contents page `project.contents` generates** (`generate: toc`) where contents put it: it stopped only the template's. The book has no contents page now, and the run says so; a sample PDF with no contents had needed a copy of `project.yaml` without the entry.
- **The table census took a table of nothing but pictures for a data table** in a page Pandoc reads. It reads a picture's alt text as its cell's text, rightly where pictures head a table's rows or columns, so such a table had text, and its rule for a table of pictures, which is layout, never fired: *Instrumental Analysis*'s 28.6, two pictures side by side, which the filter makes a figure, was listed as a data table with a header column. A table whose cells hold nothing but pictures is layout now, whatever their alt text says (`view_from_pandoc` in `lib/tablecensus.py`).
- **A target's archive held the run's intermediates.** A target whose filter settings differ from the first target's keeps its filtered pages in an `intermediates` folder inside its own, and `archive: zip` or `tar.gz` packed them with the output: *Instrumental Analysis*'s zip of Word files held the 194 pages' JSON beside the 194 Word files, 10.5 MB of it before compression. The archive holds the output alone now.
- **A formula on a MathJax site that an editor broke with a `<br>` was left as text**, its delimiters and TeX on the page: MathJax reads the text on either side of a `<br>`, a `<wbr>`, or a comment as one (FindTeX, with `includeHtmlTags`' default), and `unpack-site.py` looked for a formula in one stretch of text at a time. Such a break inside a formula is joined into it now, a `<br>` as a line end. *Instrumental Analysis* had 10, long reactions in tables broken after their `\\`. And a blank line in a formula's TeX, which MathJax reads as a space and LaTeX as a paragraph's end, stopping the PDF inside the formula ("Missing $ inserted"), is one line end, as a page's HTML laid out across lines may hold one and a formula joined across a `<br>` did.
- **A file a page asked for by a name with an extension, which came by a redirect to one without, was saved without one.** `unpack-site.py` names a file after the address that held it, and LibreTexts serves `.../files/187981/Figure12.72.png` from a signed address ending `/0/webview`, so 413 of the 522 files of *Instrumental Analysis*'s pages were written as `webview-ab616dd4` and the like, and which image a capture's redirect named so differed between captures. Such a file takes the name the page asked for it by now, and one with no extension anywhere takes the one its bytes call for.
- **A PDF stopped on a figure in a table's cell whose caption carries its own label** ("Misplaced \noalign"). The group that keeps LaTeX from numbering such a caption again calls the caption package's `\captionsetup`, which inside a longtable sets its options between the table's rows, where nothing can go from inside a cell. A figure or a table in a cell is no float, and the writer sets its caption as a paragraph LaTeX doesn't number, so it's left as it is now. *Instrumental Analysis*'s page 26.1 sets two figures side by side in a table.
- **A PDF's image with a width wider than its line ran past the page's edge.** Pandoc's LaTeX writer scales an image with no size to fit its line and the page (`\pandocbounded`), and sets one with a width or a height at that size, which a page's `width="1108"` is, at 96 pixels an inch. LibreTexts gives every picture its width in pixels: 217 of *Instrumental Analysis*'s were wider than the line, the widest 831 points against a line of 345, and 82 ran past the page's edge. Such an image is scaled as one with no size is now, and one that fits is left as it is.
- **A PDF's figure holding figures in a div, as LibreTexts sets two pictures side by side** (`<figure><div class="row"><figure>…`), **had a caption for each, empty, beside its own**, three `Caption`s in one figure, which PDF/UA-2 forbids (veraPDF's 8.2.5.27-1, on eight of *Instrumental Analysis*'s figures). A figure inside a figure was already set as its contents, its caption a paragraph under them, as a figure inside a div inside one is now.
- **An unquoted `on` or `off` stopped the run**: YAML reads `menu: on`, as the docs write it, as the boolean true, and a setting whose values are `on` and `off` took it for neither ("menu is True, which is not one of on, off"). It's the word now, as it is in quotes. A setting of other fixed values refuses a boolean, saying to quote it, as a text setting does, where it said the boolean wasn't one of its values. And `merge`'s own `off` was the boolean in the schema, so the settings reference listed its values as `False` and `groups`. [Two YAML traps](docs/configuration.md#two-yaml-traps).
- **A word or a unit in an HTML page's formula came out as italic letters**, which a screen reader spelled out: Pandoc's MathML reader reads an `<mi>` of more than one letter as letters, each written back as an identifier of its own, where MathML sets it whole and upright, as the page shows it. *Principles of Marketing*'s "Value = Benefits/Price" did, and so did units such as `MeV` and `mol`. An `<mi>` of more than two letters, and of letters and spaces alone, is read as text now (`\text{Value}`), in a page's own MathML and in what a MathJax rendering gives up, and so is one of two that the page sets upright (`mathvariant="normal"`), as a unit is and a product of variables isn't, or applies as a function (the Polish `tg`); any other of two letters, as likely a product (`dx`) as a unit (`cm`), a name Pandoc keeps whole as an operator (`sin`), one letter repeated (`xxxxx`), and one styled other than normal or bold are left as Pandoc reads them. In sixteen of OpenStax's math and physics books and *Marketing*, 1,528 identifiers of more than two letters in 1,130 formulas are text now; of two letters, in all of OpenStax's public repositories, 4,171 are, nearly all *Fizyka dla szkół wyższych*'s units and elements' symbols, and 988 are still letters, products most of them. [HTML sources](docs/html.md#what-is-read-and-what-isnt).
- **A heading inside a list, a quotation, a table, a figure, or a footnote broke a tagged PDF's structure**: Pandoc's LaTeX writer makes it a sectioning command where it stands (`\paragraph` in an `\item`, as OpenStax puts a note with a title in a list's item), and LaTeX's tagging can't close the section it opens inside the environment, so every section after it was tagged inside the list, 27 of *Principles of Marketing*'s, which PDF/UA-2 rejects (veraPDF's `L-Sect` and `LBody-LI`). It's a paragraph now, bold, kept with what follows, and tagged as the heading it is (`H1` to `H6` by its level), its id an anchor a link goes to; in a footnote, which holds no heading, a bold paragraph. Such a heading isn't in the contents or the bookmarks, as a section is.
- **A PDF's table whose cells, side by side, were longer than a line ran past the page's edge**, where its columns had no widths and each of its cells was one paragraph, as an HTML page's table without a `<colgroup>` is: Pandoc's writer sets such a table in columns that don't wrap. In *Prealgebra 2e*'s chapter 3, 25 tables did, a worked example's steps most of them, by up to 258 points, and none does now: such a table is laid out as LaTeX measures it, as a worked example's steps are, each column as wide as its widest cell where the table fits its line, and otherwise wrapping, no narrower than its longest word or formula, and a formula broken only where the table won't fit its line with it whole. The count of characters that had given such a table its widths, as it still does for LaTeX's first run and for a TeX without Lua, takes a fraction's two parts one after the other, and its columns broke formulas the line had room for whole: in *College Algebra 2e*'s chapter 3, the toolkit functions' √x and ∛x after their equals signs, and in *Precalculus 2e*'s chapter 4, a row's label `f(x) = 2^x`, beside pairs of numbers with fractions; and a fill-in blank too long for the line, where the writer's columns don't wrap, set its table in smaller type. Words beside a long blank keep their line where, as LaTeX measures them, they fit it with the blank at its least, three underscores, or two thirds of its column after words on its line, as its label, the blank drawn in the rest: counted whole, the blank had taken the line from them, and they wrapped. A table with nothing to wrap, as a table of numbers is, keeps the writer's columns. One whose longest words alone are wider than a line is set in smaller type (below).
- **A PDF numbered a table with an id and no caption, and lost the id of one it doesn't number.** Pandoc's LaTeX writer holds such a table's id in an empty caption, `\caption{}\label{…}`, which LaTeX numbers, so "Table 1" stood over a table the book never numbered, an HTML page's `<table id>` with no `<caption>`, say, and a link to a table of class `unnumbered`, which gets no caption and so no `\label`, went nowhere. The id is an anchor before the table now, as a layout table's is.
- **A link to a Word table of one cell went nowhere**, a box OpenStax's export bookmarks as it does every table, between blocks, where Pandoc's reader drops a bookmark: the header pre-pass hands the filter the bookmarks before a table, and it did so only for a table it lists, which a box of one cell, or a table of pictures alone, isn't. It hands over those too now, and still doesn't list the table. *Clinical Nursing Skills*' link to its Table 25.5, a muscle-strength scale in one cell, lands now, on the page and in the EPUB.
- **A Word table whose first cell held a no-break space, two paragraphs, or a formula took no declaration**, the header guess's or a row of `table-headers.csv`'s, and lost the bookmarks before it, which links to the table go to. The filter finds each table the header pre-pass listed by its shape and its first cell's first 20 characters, and it read the cell otherwise than the pre-pass does: a no-break space as other than a space, since Lua's `%s` doesn't match one, the cell's paragraphs run together, and a formula as its TeX, where the pre-pass has its letters (`x_{n}` and `xn`). It reads a cell as the pre-pass does now, and compares the words before a formula. Seven tables in the test corpus were set aside so, each with a warning, and links to four of the five checked again went nowhere, on the page and in the EPUB; none does now. The warning is given once for a table, where it was given twice, and says what isn't applied.
- **The header guess took a negative number written with a minus sign for a word.** A cell is a number to the guess when it matches `NUMERIC` (`lib/tablecensus.py`), which took a hyphen for a negative's sign but not the minus sign (U+2212), which OpenStax's books write one with, as the patterns for a quantity and an amount already did: a top row of such numbers could read as a header row, and a column of them as labels whose order wasn't read. It's a number now, its sign counted in a column's order. On the seven books of the Word corpus (2,943 tables) no guess changes; on the 67 books unpacked from OpenStax's repositories (12,221 tables), one: *Contemporary Mathematics*' table of numbers and whether each is an integer, whose first column, −3 among its numbers, was read as labels (`first-column`) and is read as numbers now (`unknown`).
- **A numbered list inside a list's item that starts past its first number came back from a Markdown target as text**: `b.` under `5.`, or `3.` under `B.` where a list goes on from a page or a slide before, and so did one in a definition. Since 3.10.1, Pandoc's Markdown reader takes a list inside an item, or a definition, only when it starts at its first number, though its writer still writes the list's own start, so reading the Markdown back made the list's items paragraphs of the item above. The target writes such a list from its first number now, its own start an empty span at its first item, `1.  []{start="3"}`, which the reading filter puts back. Found by a deck's round trip, five of whose slides continue a list from the slide before.
- **A formula in a deck's speaker notes was its TeX in the PowerPoint file.** Pandoc's PowerPoint writer writes a formula in the notes as its TeX, `\hat{y}=a+bx`, since the notes hold no equation. The `pptx` target writes it as the Unicode text Pandoc's plain writer makes of it, `ŷ = a + bx`, with a word's spaces and a script it has no character for as a deck types one (`x̄_n`), which `math.from_text` reads back as the formula; one texmath can't write as text, a fraction, is left its TeX, and the run counts both. [The PowerPoint file](docs/slides.md#the-powerpoint-file).
- **A letter with a hat or a bar typed as text stayed text.** OpenStax's statistics decks type ŷ and x̄ in their notes as a letter and a combining mark, "the regression line ŷ = a + bx" and "the running average (x̄_n)", which NVDA reads wrong as text. `math.from_text` takes such a letter, or a precomposed `ŷ`, standing alone as a variable, and a symbol on its own, a script after it typed as TeX types one (`_n`) its script, and in a run holding one the plain letters between its operators as variables too, so both are equations now, `\hat{y}=a+bx` and `\bar{x}_{n}`; a letter with a hat in a word (`crêpe`) stays the word's, and a run without one keeps its plain letters text (`a + b = c`). A book's pages are read the same way. [Math](docs/configuration.md#math).
- **`math.from_text` left a plain letter out of the expression its subscript was in, and took a label's number into one.** Typed with a plain H, `H₀: σ₁² = σ₂²` became an equation from its subscript on, a subscript on nothing, the H left before it as text; and `Plan 1: n₁ = 4` became the equation `1:n₁ = 4`, the label's number and colon in it. A single plain letter right before a sub- or superscript is the letter it's set on now, which the expression takes (though no symbol on its own, so `H₂O` stays text, as does a formula whose script runs into a letter, `2H₂ + O₂ = 2H₂O`), and a colon and a space end a run with no variable yet. On OpenStax's 13 statistics decks, it's the nine such expressions on the ANOVA deck's slides, all right now, and nothing else changes. A book's pages are read the same way: a page's `S<sub>mb</sub> = 0`, which had stayed text for want of a variable, is the formula `S_{mb} = 0` now.
- **Emphasis that ended in a line break came back from a Markdown or an AsciiDoc target as text**, and so did bold, underline, strikethrough, a superscript, or a subscript, and in AsciiDoc small caps. Pandoc's Markdown writer closes such a mark after the break's backslash, `*for any\*`, which its reader takes for an escaped asterisk, so the mark was lost and its asterisks were text; its AsciiDoc writer puts its ` +` inside the mark, where it isn't at the end of its line, so its reader keeps it as a plus. Both targets close the mark before the break now, a break at the end of a span with no attributes that ends the mark included, which each writer writes as its reader reads it back (measured, Pandoc 3.12.1). A superscript or a subscript with a break anywhere in it, which neither reader takes back, a script holding no break, is cut at the break, `x^a^\` and `^b^` on the next line. In AsciiDoc a break at a mark's start goes before the mark, which can't open on the break's space (`_ +`, read back as text), and a paragraph that starts with a break, one a mark gave up or not, has `{empty}` before it, where its ` +` alone on its line had made the paragraph a literal one. Found by a deck's round trip.
- **A table its page marks as a layout table was treated as a data table.** With `role="presentation"`, the header guess and the output check left it alone, but the filter asked a description of it in `table-captions-missing.csv`, warned that it had no header row, and wrapped it in a focusable scroll region; the PDF tagged it a Table, and a Markdown target lost the role, so it read back a data table. With `role="none"`, the output check reported it as a table without headers or a caption, and outside a scroll region, besides. DCIC's derivations, which its site profile marks as layout tables, were among them.
- **A Word or PowerPoint copy left a middle dot in an equation as typed**, where the pages make it the product's dot operator (`\cdot`): a `format: source` copy, and `remediate.py --repair-equations`, write the dot operator (U+22C5) now, as Pandoc writes `\cdot` in an equation, and leave a middle dot in an equation's normal text as the pages leave it in `\text{}`.
- **A split table's part had a colon after a caption that ends a sentence** ("…only one value per field.: Beginning Data", in *Foundations of Information Systems*): the part's words follow such a caption after a space now.
- **A link to a file named in another case, or with backslashes, stopped the run.** A page's link to a file beside it, a PDF or a spreadsheet the page offers, which a target copies as it does an image, wasn't copied when it named the file in another case than the file's (`docs/handout.PDF`, the file `Docs/Handout.pdf`), as macOS and Windows find it, or with Windows's backslashes (`Data\Results.csv`), and the packager stopped the run ("referenced file(s) are not on disk"). Such a link is read as the file now, its fragment kept, and the run names it, as it did an image named so: from a Markdown, HTML, AsciiDoc, or Word source, and a target's variant of a page, an HTML variant's images too, which had been read as written. Changing the source is still the fix.
- **A footnote in a figure's caption took a second number in a PDF.** A tagged PDF's figure caption is set twice, once in a box to measure it (`latex-lab-testphase-float.sty`'s `\@makecaption`), as an untagged one too long for its line is where the caption package isn't loaded (a book with no table), and the note's mark stepped the footnote counter each time, so the note took the second number and the first went to none ("Figure 1: A caption.3" for the book's second note). Such a caption opens with `\OERCaptionNotes` now (`pdf-target.lua`, `latexbuild.py`), which sets the counter back each time the caption is set after the first, and the list of figures takes the caption without it.
- **A footnote in a table inside a table's cell was a minipage's in a PDF**: its mark a letter ("a"), and its text numbered as the note before it was. It's numbered among the outer table's notes now, its text after the outer table with theirs, each mark linked to its note.
